<div class='question question'>

Work out $3024 \div 9$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3024 \div 9 \\\\
&= \dfrac{3024}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{336 \times 9}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{336 \times \cancel 9}{\cancel 9} \\\\
&= 336
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$336$

</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

&= \dfrac{336 \times 9}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{336 \times \cancel 9}{\cancel 9} \\\\
&= 336
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A third of a certain number is $27$.

What is the number? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {3} \times a  &= 27 \\\\
a                        &= 27 \times 3 \\\\
a                        &= 81
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$81$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>7AD11D07-8452-4D33-A58B-2F361B96929A</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2:1:8</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tsequence
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Write a number in the box to complete the number sequence below:

$94 \quad \boxed{\phantom{11}} \quad 80 \quad 73 \quad 66$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by subtracting $7$ from the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\maroonC {(-7)} } 
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(-7)} }  
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(-7)} }   
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(-7)} }  \\
94,  &&    \ldots,  &&  80,  &&   73, && 66
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Missing number}        &= 94 - 7 \\\\
                             &= 87
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$87$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The next term is calculated by subtracting $7$ from the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{matrix}

$\dfrac{1}{5} \quad \dfrac{3}{7} \quad \dfrac{5}{9} \quad \dfrac{7}{11}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$\dfrac {1} {2} = 0.5$

Let's convert all the numbers into decimals.

$
\begin {aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {5}, \quad \dfrac {3} {7}, \quad \dfrac {5} {9}, \quad \dfrac {7} {11} \\\\
&= 0.2, \quad 0.42, \quad 0.55, \quad 0.63
\end {aligned}
$

Closest number $= 0.55 = \dfrac {5} {9}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {5} {9}$

</div>
</div>

\end {aligned}
$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$\boxed{\phantom{11}}  \ \times 16 = 36 \div 9$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 16  &= 36 \div 9 \\\\
a \times 16  &= \dfrac {36} {9} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {36} {9 \times 16} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {9 \times 4 \times 4} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {9} \times 4 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {4}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 16  &= 36 \div 9 \\\\
a \times 16  &= \dfrac {36} {9} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {36} {9 \times 16} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {9 \times 4 \times 4} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {9} \times 4 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {4}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>6DE9A7A7-AD34-403D-8860-9E22A51BF727</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2:1:14</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Lara and Cathy have each thought of a positive whole number less than $20$.
The product of their numbers is $56$ and the difference between them is $10$.


What are their two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number that Lara thought $= a$

Let the number that Cathy thought $= b$

As per the question,

$a \times b = 56$

$a - b = 10$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- Both the numbers should be smaller than $20$
- The product of the $2$ numbers is $56$ 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $10$.

Factors of $56 = 1, 2, 4, 7, 8, 14, 28, 56$

Factor pairs of $56$ that are smaller than $20 = (4, 14) \ \text {and} \ (7, 8)$

| Factors   |  Difference  | Numbers $< 20$    |
|:-------:  |:-----------: |:---------------:  |
| $4, 14$   |   $10$       |       Yes         |
| $7,  8$   |   $1$        |       Yes         |

A quick check shows that $4$ and $14$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two numbers $= 4,14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4, 14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number that Lara thought $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the number that Cathy thought $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$a \times b = 56$
<emptyline>
$a - b = 10$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.
<emptyline>
- Both the numbers should be smaller than $20$
- The product of the $2$ numbers is $56$ 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $10$.
<emptyline>
Factors of $56 = 1, 2, 4, 7, 8, 14, 28, 56$
<emptyline>
Factor pairs of $56$ that are smaller than $20 = (4, 14) \ \text {and} \ (7, 8)$
<emptyline>
| Factors   |  Difference  | Numbers $< 20$    |
|:-------:  |:-----------: |:---------------:  |
| $4, 14$   |   $10$       |       Yes         |
| $7,  8$   |   $1$        |       Yes         |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $4$ and $14$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. rtq_abbr_md_quadratic_note*
<emptyline>
The two numbers $= 4,14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4, 14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

Amy has the two-stage number machine shown below.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-15-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-15-subquestion-1-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-15-subquestion-1-00.png")

Complete the table of input and output numbers for Amy's machine.

| **input** | **output** |
|:---------:|:----------:|
|    $2$    |     $0$    |
|    $5$    |            |
|           |    $-2$    |
|   $-2$    |            |

</div>
<div class='workings'>

|:-------------------------------------------------------:                                                |:-------------------------------------------------------------:                                                |

|                         $5$                                                                             | $\green{(5 - 2) \times 2 = 3 \times 2 = 6} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(1)}}$      |
| $\green{\dfrac {-2} {2} + 2 = -1 + 2 = 1} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(2)}}$ |                      $-2$                                                                                     |
|                        $-2$                                                                             | $\green{(-2 - 2) \times 2 = (-4) \times 2 = -8} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(3)}}$ |

| **input** | **output** |
|:---------:|:----------:|
|    $2$    |     $0$    |
|    $5$    |     $6$    |
|   $1$     |    $-2$    |
|   $-2$    |    $-8$    |

|                        $-2$                                                                             | $\rtq_katex_color_answer{(-2 - 2) \times 2 = (-4) \times 2 = -8} \ \rtq_katex_color_info{\footnotesize{(3)}}$ |

<div class='question subquestion'>

Hannah has a different number machine which has produced the following table of input and output numbers.

| **input** | **output** |
|:---------:|:----------:|
|  $-1$     |    $-2$    |
|   $0$     |    $-2$    |
|   $1$     |    $4$     |
|   $3$     |    $10$    |

Unfortunately the labels have fallen off Hannah's machine.

Write suitable labels on the diagram below.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-15-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-15-subquestion-2-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-15-subquestion-2-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$\text{Input} \times 3 + 1 = \text{Output}$

Let's verify.

| **input** |             **output**              |
|:---------:|:-----------------------------------:|
|  $-1$     |    $-1 \times 3 + 1 = -3 + 1 = -2$  |
|   $0$     |    $0 \times 3 + 1 = 0 + 1 = 1$     |
|   $1$     |    $1 \times 3 + 1 = 3 + 1 = 4$     |
|   $3$     |    $3 \times 3 + 1 = 9 + 1 = 10$    |

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text{Input} \times 3 + 1 = \text{Output}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\text{Input} \times 3 + 1 = \text{Output}$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
| **input** |             **output**              |
|:---------:|:-----------------------------------:|
|  $-1$     |    $-1 \times 3 + 1 = -3 + 1 = -2$  |
|   $0$     |    $0 \times 3 + 1 = 0 + 1 = 1$     |
|   $1$     |    $1 \times 3 + 1 = 3 + 1 = 4$     |
|   $3$     |    $3 \times 3 + 1 = 9 + 1 = 10$    |
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2:1:16</p>

ttime
</li>

<div class='question question'>

Katy is going to the cinema to see a film that starts at $3.25 \ \text{pm}$ and lasts $108 \ \text{minutes}$.

At what time will the film end?

Write your answer using the $24{-}\text{hour}$ clock.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Ending time}     &= 3{:}25 + 108 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                       &= 15{:}25 + 108 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                       &= 15{:}25 + 1 \ \text{hour} \ 48 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                       &= 17{:}13
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17{:}13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Ending time}     &= 3{:}25 + 108 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                       &= 15{:}25 + 108 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                       &= 15{:}25 + 1 \ \text{hour} \ 48 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                       &= 17{:}13
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$17{:}13$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>F922D651-408E-4359-84E9-92753CFE0F39</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2:1:17</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A shop sells coffee from Ethiopia.

The prices for coffee beans and ground coffee are shown below.

|coffee beans |ground coffee|
|:-------:|:----------:|
|$\pounds 9.40 \ \text{per kg}$| $\pounds 2.10 \ \text{per} \ 100\text{g}$|

How much more does it cost to buy $250 \text{g}$ of ground coffee than $250 \text{g}$ of coffee beans?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of ground coffee = a

Let the cost of coffee beans = b

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a                               &= \dfrac {2.10} {100} \times 250 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2.10 \times 250} {100} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2.10 \times 5 \times 50} {2 \times 50} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2.10 \times 5 \times \cancel {50}} {2 \times \cancel {50}} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {10.5} {2} \\\\
a                               &= \pounds 5.250 \\\\
b                               &= \dfrac {9.40} {1000} \times 250 \\\\
b                               &= \dfrac {9.40 \times 250} {1000} \\\\
b                               &= \dfrac {9.40 \times 250 } {250 \times 4} \\\\
b                               &= \dfrac {9.40 \times \cancel{250} } {\cancel {250} \times 4} \\\\
b                               &= \dfrac {9.40} {4} \\\\
b                               &= \pounds 2.350
\end {aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference in cost} &= a - b \\\\
                          &= 5.250 - 2.350 \\\\
                          &= \pounds 2.900
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 2.900$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of ground coffee = a
<emptyline>
Let the cost of coffee beans = b
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a                               &= \dfrac {2.10} {100} \times 250 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2.10 \times 250} {100} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2.10 \times 5 \times 50} {2 \times 50} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2.10 \times 5 \times \cancel {50}} {2 \times \cancel {50}} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {10.5} {2} \\\\
a                               &= \pounds 5.250 \\\\
b                               &= \dfrac {9.40} {1000} \times 250 \\\\
b                               &= \dfrac {9.40 \times 250} {1000} \\\\
b                               &= \dfrac {9.40 \times 250 } {250 \times 4} \\\\
b                               &= \dfrac {9.40 \times \cancel{250} } {\cancel {250} \times 4} \\\\
b                               &= \dfrac {9.40} {4} \\\\
b                               &= \pounds 2.350
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference in cost} &= a - b \\\\
                          &= 5.250 - 2.350 \\\\
                          &= \pounds 2.900
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 2.900$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Sandy needs $12$ pizzas to feed $30$ people.

How many pizzas will she need to feed $35$ people?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of pizza for one person $=a$

Number of pizza to feed 35 people $= 35 a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a                              &= \dfrac {12} {30} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {3 \times 4} {3 \times 10} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 4} {\cancel 3 \times 10} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {4}{10} \\\\
a                              &= 0.4 
\end{aligned}
$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pizza to feed 35 people} &= 35 \times a \\\\
                                         &= 35 \times 0.4 \\\\
                                         &= 14 
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of pizza for one person $=a$
<emptyline>
Number of pizza to feed 35 people $= 35 a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a                              &= \dfrac {12} {30} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {3 \times 4} {3 \times 10} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 4} {\cancel 3 \times 10} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {4}{10} \\\\
a                              &= 0.4 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pizza to feed 35 people} &= 35 \times a \\\\
                                         &= 35 \times 0.4 \\\\
                                         &= 14 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2:1:19</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>

talgebra

<div class='question question'>

Belinda ate $\dfrac{3}{5}$ of a bar of chocolate.

$60 \ \text{grams}$ of chocolate remained.

What was the original mass of the chocolate bar?

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-19-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-19-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-19-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the original mass of the chocolate bar $= a \ \text {grams}$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3 a} {5} + 60                                   &= a \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{3 a}{5} + 60 \\\\
a - \dfrac {3 a} {5}                                    &= 60 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 5} {1 \times 5} - \dfrac {3 a} {5}     &= 60 \\\\
\dfrac {5 a} {5} - \dfrac {3 a} {5}                     &= 60 \\\\
\dfrac {5 a - 3 a} {5}                                  &= 60 \\\\
\dfrac {2 a} {5}                                        &= 60 \\\\
2 a                                                     &= 60 \times 5 \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {60 \times 5} {2} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 30 \times 5} {2} \\\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 30 \times 5} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                                                       &= 30 \times 5 \\\\
a                                                       &= 150 \ \text {grams}
\end{aligned}
$

Original mass of chocolate $= 150 \text { grams}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$150 \ \text {grams}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the original mass of the chocolate bar $= a \ \text {grams}$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3 a} {5} + 60                                   &= a \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{3 a}{5} + 60 \\\\
a - \dfrac {3 a} {5}                                    &= 60 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 5} {1 \times 5} - \dfrac {3 a} {5}     &= 60 \\\\
\dfrac {5 a} {5} - \dfrac {3 a} {5}                     &= 60 \\\\
\dfrac {5 a - 3 a} {5}                                  &= 60 \\\\
\dfrac {2 a} {5}                                        &= 60 \\\\
2 a                                                     &= 60 \times 5 \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {60 \times 5} {2} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 30 \times 5} {2} \\\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 30 \times 5} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                                                       &= 30 \times 5 \\\\
a                                                       &= 150 \ \text {grams}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Original mass of chocolate $= 150 \text { grams}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$150 \ \text {grams}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Callum buys a new laptop costing $\pounds 572.50$.

He makes an initial payment of $\pounds 136$, and then pays the remainder in $6$ equal monthly amounts.

How much is each monthly amount?

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-21-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-21-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-21-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let the monthly amount = a 

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a                                &= \dfrac {572.50 - 136} {6} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {436.5} {6} \\\\
a                                &= \pounds 72.75
\end{aligned}
$

Monthly amount $= \pounds 72.25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 72.75$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let the monthly amount = a 
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a                                &= \dfrac {572.50 - 136} {6} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {436.5} {6} \\\\
a                                &= \pounds 72.75
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Monthly amount $= \pounds 72.25$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 72.75$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

\end {aligned}
$

\end {aligned}
$

<div class='answer'>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Use the information below to complete the timetable.

Each train stops at all of the stations shown.

Train $1$ takes $57 \ \text{minutes}$ to get from Spongeton to Squareford.

Each train takes the same amount of time to travel from Bobville to Pantsbridge.

Train $2$ takes twice as long to travel from Bobville to Squareford as from Spongeton to Bobville.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
{} & \text{Spongeton} & \text{Bobville} & \text{Squareford} & \text{Pantsbridge} \\
\text{train} \ 1  &   10{:}45   &   11{:}10   &   11{:}42 &  12{:}04 \\
\text{train} \ 2  &   11{:}30   &   11{:}46   &   12{:}18 &  12{:}40 \\
\text{train} \ 3  &   12{:}15   &   12{:}51   &   13{:}22 &  13{:}45 
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Which of these trains takes the longest to travel from Spongeton to Pantsbridge?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken by train} \ 1  &= 12{:}04 - 10{:}45 \\\\
                                &= 1 \ \text{hour} \ 19 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken by train} \ 2  &= 12{:}40 - 11{:}30 \\\\
                                &= 1 \ \text{hour} \ 10 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken by train} \ 3  &= 13{:}45 - 12{:}15 \\\\
                                &= 1 \ \text{hour} \ 30 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

$ \text{Train} \ 3 \ \text{takes the longest}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$ \text{Train} \ 3 \ \text{takes the longest}$

</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Di and Ed make up two games, based on Di's results.
Tick the relevant box in the table below to say who is most likely to win, or whether there is an equal chance.

| game | rules                                                                                        | Di most likely to win | Ed most likely win | equally likely |
|:----:|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------|--------------------|----------------|
|   A  | Di scores a point if the total thrown is even. <br> Ed scores a point if the total thrown is odd. |                       |                    |                |
|   B  | Di scores a point if the total thrown is $8$ to $12$ inclusive. <br> Ed scores a point otherwise.     |                       |                    |                |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Di chances of winning game A}  &= \dfrac {3+10+21+36+28+15+6+1} {240} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {120} {240} \\\\
                                &= 0.5 \\\\
\text{Di chances of winning game B}  &= \dfrac {21+28+36+36+28} {240} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {149} {240} \\\\
                                &= 0.62
\end{aligned}
$

| game | Di most likely to win | Ed most likely win | equally likely |
|:----:|:---------------------:|:------------------:|:--------------:|
|   A  |          No           |        No          |      Yes       |
|   B  |          Yes          |        No          |      No        |

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Di chances of winning game A}  &= \dfrac {3+10+21+36+28+15+6+1} {240} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {120} {240} \\\\
                                &= 0.5 \\\\
\text{Di chances of winning game B}  &= \dfrac {21+28+36+36+28} {240} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {149} {240} \\\\
                                &= 0.62
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
| game | Di most likely to win | Ed most likely win | equally likely |
|:----:|:---------------------:|:------------------:|:--------------:|
|   A  |          No           |        No          |      Yes       |
|   B  |          Yes          |        No          |      No        |
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

In the diagrams below, the perimeter of the square is equal to the perimeter of the isosceles triangle.

Work out the length of the side marked $d$.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-32-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-32-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-32-00.png")
![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-32-01.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-32-01.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-32-01.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of triangle} = \text{Sum of all sides}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Perimeter of the square}     &= 4 \times \text {Sides} \\\\
                                    &= 4 \times 9 \\\\
                                    &= 36 \ \text {cm}
\end {aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Perimeter of the triangle}   &= 36 \\\\
d + d + 20                          &= 36 \\\\
2d + 20                             &= 36 \\\\
2d                                  &= 36 - 20 \\\\
2d                                  &= 16 \\\\
d                                   &= \dfrac {16} {2} \\\\
d                                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 2} {2} \\\\
d                                   &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
d                                   &= 8 \ \text {cm}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_square
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_triangle
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Perimeter of the square}     &= 4 \times \text {Sides} \\\\
                                    &= 4 \times 9 \\\\
                                    &= 36 \ \text {cm}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Perimeter of the triangle}   &= 36 \\\\
d + d + 20                          &= 36 \\\\
2d + 20                             &= 36 \\\\
2d                                  &= 36 - 20 \\\\
2d                                  &= 16 \\\\
d                                   &= \dfrac {16} {2} \\\\
d                                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 2} {2} \\\\
d                                   &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
d                                   &= 8 \ \text {cm}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Six members of the Clark family have the following shoe sizes:

$9 \quad 1 \dfrac{1}{2} \quad 5 \quad 1 \dfrac{1}{2} \quad 3 \quad 4$

For the Clark family, work out:

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subsubquestion lower-roman question_type_subsubquestion list_type_lower-roman'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

the median shoe size

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the shoe sizes in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Shoe sizes}             &= 9, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 5, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 3, \quad 4 \\\\
\text{Ordered shoe sizes}     &= 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad \maroonC{3}, \quad \maroonC{4}, \quad 5, \quad 9 \\\\
\text{Median shoe size}       &= \dfrac {3 + 4} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7} {2} \\\\
                              &= 3 \dfrac {1} {2}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \dfrac {1} {2}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>
Let's arrange the shoe sizes in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Shoe sizes}             &= 9, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 5, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 3, \quad 4 \\\\
\text{Ordered shoe sizes}     &= 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{3}, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{4}, \quad 5, \quad 9 \\\\
\text{Median shoe size}       &= \dfrac {3 + 4} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7} {2} \\\\
                              &= 3 \dfrac {1} {2}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3 \dfrac {1} {2}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

the mean shoe size

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean shoe size}   &= \dfrac {1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {(\dfrac {3} {2} + \dfrac {3} {2}) + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 4} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean shoe size}   &= \dfrac {1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {(\dfrac {3} {2} + \dfrac {3} {2}) + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 4} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>
</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Jane has recorded the shoe size of every girl in her class.

Her results are shown in the table below.

|shoe size        |$3$|$4$ |$5$|$6$|
|:---------------:|:-:|:--:|:-:|:-:|
|number of people |$2$|$12$|$7$|$1$|

Jane says “The mode is $12$”.

Annabel says “The mode is $4$”.

Who is correct? Give a reason for your answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Annabel is correct. $4$ is the most common shoe size and hence the mode. $12$ is the count of girls who has that shoe size.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Annabel

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Annabel is correct. $4$ is the most common shoe size and hence the mode. $12$ is the count of girls who has that shoe size.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Annabel
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

In a quiz with $20$ questions, contestants are awarded $3$ points for a correct answer, but lose $2$ points for each wrong answer.

Complete the table for the contestants below.

The first one has already been done for you.

|   name  | number of correct answers | number of wrong answers | total points |
|:-------:|:-------------------------:|:-----------------------:|:------------:|
| Rebecca |            $7$            |           $13$          |     $-5$     |
|  Safiya |            $14$           |                         |              |
|  Tosin  |                           |           $16$          |              |
|  Ursula |                           |                         |      $0$     |


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of correct answers by Ursula $= a$

Number of wrong answers by Ursula $= 20 - a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 3 - (20 - a) \times 2          &= 0 \\\\
3a - 20 \times 2 + 2a                   &= 0 \\\\
5a - 40                                 &= 0 \\\\
5a                                      &= 40 \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac {40} {5} \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 8} {5} \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 8} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                       &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

Let's complete the table.

|   name  | number of correct answers                                                          | number of wrong answers                                                             | total points                                                                                                    |
|:-------:|:----------------------------------:                                                |:-----------------------------------:                                                |:---------------------------------------------------------------:                                                |
| Rebecca |            $7$                                                                     |           $13$                                                                      |          $-5$                                                                                                   |
|  Safiya |            $14$                                                                    | $\green{20 - 14 = 6} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(2)}}$  | $\green{14 \times 3 - 6 \times 2 = 42 - 12 = 30} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(3)}}$  |
|  Tosin  | $\green{20 - 16 = 4} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(1)}}$ |           $16$                                                                      | $\green{4 \times 3 - 16 \times 2 = 12 - 32 = -20} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(4)}}$ |
|  Ursula |      $\green{8} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(5)}}$      |  $\green{20 - 8 = 12} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(6)}}$ |          $0$                                                                                                    |

Completed table:

|   name  | number of correct answers | number of wrong answers | total points |
|:-------:|:-------------------------:|:-----------------------:|:------------:|
| Rebecca |            $7$            |           $13$          |     $-5$     |
|  Safiya |            $14$           |           $6$           |     $30$     |
|  Tosin  |           $4$             |           $16$          |     $-20$    |
|  Ursula |           $8$             |           $12$          |      $0$     |

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of correct answers by Ursula $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of wrong answers by Ursula $= 20 - a$

$

<div class='question question'>

The shape below is made from two overlapping rectangles.

One rectangle measures $5 \ \text{cm}$ by $4 \ \text{cm}$.

The other measures $8 \ \text{cm}$ by $6 \ \text{cm}$.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png")

Given that the area of the section shaded black is $15 \ \text{cm}^2$, work out the area of the section shaded grey.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of grey section}            &= \text {Area of larger rectangle} - (\text {Area of smaller rectangle} - \text {Area of black section}) \\\\
                                        &= 6 \times 8 - (4 \times 5 - 15) \\\\
                                        &= 48 - (20 - 15) \\\\
                                        &= 48 - 5 \\\\
                                        &= 43 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$43 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of grey section}            &= \text {Area of larger rectangle} - (\text {Area of smaller rectangle} - \text {Area of black section}) \\\\
                                        &= 6 \times 8 - (4 \times 5 - 15) \\\\
                                        &= 48 - (20 - 15) \\\\
                                        &= 48 - 5 \\\\
                                        &= 43 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$43 \ \text {cm}^2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Maddie spent the same amount on pineapples as she did on mangoes.

Given that she bought at least one of each fruit, what is the smallest amount she could have spent?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of pineapples $= a$

Let the number of mangoes $= b$

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 1.40 \times a               &= 80 \ \text{p} \times b \\\\
1.40 \times 100 \ \text{p} \times a   &= 80 \ \text{p} \times b \\\\
140 \times a                        &= 80 \times b \\\\
80 \times b                         &= 140 \times a
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which satisfies the above equation.

| Value of $a$  |  Value of $b$   |   $140a$           |    $80b$       |
|:----------:   |:-----------:    |:----------------:  |:------------:  |
| $1$           |     $2$         |        140         |    160         |
| $2$           |     $3$         |        280         |    240         |
| $5$           |     $6$         |        700         |    480         |
| $4$           |     $7$         |        560         |    560         |

A quick check shows that $4$ and $7$ meets all the criteria above.

Number of pineapples $= 4$

Number of mangoes $= 7$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Pineapples $=$ $4$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Mangoes $=$ $7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of pineapples $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the number of mangoes $= b$
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 1.40 \times a               &= 80 \ \text{p} \times b \\\\
1.40 \times 100 \ \text{p} \times a   &= 80 \ \text{p} \times b \\\\
140 \times a                        &= 80 \times b \\\\
80 \times b                         &= 140 \times a
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which satisfies the above equation.
<emptyline>
| Value of $a$  |  Value of $b$   |   $140a$           |    $80b$       |
|:----------:   |:-----------:    |:----------------:  |:------------:  |
| $1$           |     $2$         |        140         |    160         |
| $2$           |     $3$         |        280         |    240         |
| $5$           |     $6$         |        700         |    480         |
| $4$           |     $7$         |        560         |    560         |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $4$ and $7$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
Number of pineapples $= 4$
<emptyline>
Number of mangoes $= 7$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Mangoes</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Belinda spends $\pounds 9$ altogether on pineapples and mangoes.

How many mangoes does she buy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of pineapples $= a$

Let the number of mangoes $= b$

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 1.40 \times a + 80\ \text{p} \times b                    &= \pounds 9 \\\\
1.40 \times 100\ \text{p} \times a + 80 \ \text{p} \times b       &= 9 \times 100 \ \text{p} \\\\
140a + 80b                                                     &= 900 \\\\
20 (7a + 4b)                                                   &= 900 \\\\
7a + 4b                                                        &= \dfrac{900} {20} \\\\
7a + 4b                                                        &= \dfrac{45 \times 2 \times 10} {2 \times 10} \\\\
7a + 4b                                                        &= \dfrac{45 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {10}} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
7a + 4b                                                        &= 45
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which satisfies the above equation.

| Value of $a$  |  Value of $b$   |    $7a +4b$          |
|:---------:    |:--------------: |:------------------:  |
| $2$           |      $4$        |       30             |
| $4$           |      $5$        |       48             |
| $3$           |      $6$        |       45             |

A quick check shows that $3$ and $6$ meets all the criteria above.

Number of mangoes $= 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of pineapples $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the number of mangoes $= b$
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 1.40 \times a + 80\ \text{p} \times b                    &= \pounds 9 \\\\
1.40 \times 100\ \text{p} \times a + 80 \ \text{p} \times b       &= 9 \times 100 \ \text{p} \\\\
140a + 80b                                                     &= 900 \\\\
20 (7a + 4b)                                                   &= 900 \\\\
7a + 4b                                                        &= \dfrac{900} {20} \\\\
7a + 4b                                                        &= \dfrac{45 \times 2 \times 10} {2 \times 10} \\\\
7a + 4b                                                        &= \dfrac{45 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {10}} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
7a + 4b                                                        &= 45
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which satisfies the above equation.
<emptyline>
| Value of $a$  |  Value of $b$   |    $7a +4b$          |
|:---------:    |:--------------: |:------------------:  |
| $2$           |      $4$        |       30             |
| $4$           |      $5$        |       48             |
| $3$           |      $6$        |       45             |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $3$ and $6$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
Number of mangoes $= 6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

This cube has been formed by joining together small cubes.

Some of the small cubes touch other cubes on exactly $3$ faces, 
some touch on exactly $4$ faces, some touch on exactly $5$ faces, and some touch on all $6$ faces.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-39-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-39-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-2/section-1-question-39-00.png")

</div>
